# Authored node revenue audit

Date: 2026-10-06. Branch `authored-revenue-audit` (from `structural-dedupe-and-owner-decisions`). Read-only audit: no code or data changed. Related: Complaints 369, 283 (closed), 335, 336.

## Why this matters

The payback floor that capped authored revenue is gone (owner decision: a new technique may earn a fortune until its own output drives its price down). That is sound only for revenue derived from output. A node with typed `rev` keeps earning that figure forever, whatever the market does, which breaks CLAUDE.md 4.1 and 4.5: revenue must fall out of what a node produces at calculated prices. The owner decision of 2026-10-02 (Complaint 283) adds that a technology does not make money by itself: what a concern makes goes into the market, and its income is what supply and demand give it. So a node that sells nothing should earn nothing (class d below).

## Counts (2026-10-06, after the hand review of class d)

Command: `python3 -c` over `sim.engine.data.load()` (script in the method section), grouping nodes by `_revenue_basis`, then the classification below.

| What | Count |
|---|---|
| nodes in the loaded tree | 2890 |
| nodes with `_revenue_basis` output (derived from entries) | 32 |
| nodes with `_revenue_basis` knowledge (sciences, earn nothing) | 10 |
| nodes with `_revenue_basis` authored (typed `rev` stands) | 1209 |
| nodes with no revenue at all (authored rev is zero) | 1639 |

Of the 1209 authored nodes, only 19 gate any production entry (and derivation stops on a missing bound); the other 1190 gate none, so there is nothing physical to derive from.

| Class | Meaning | Nodes |
|---|---|---|
| a | an entry for its own good exists; only gating or a physical bound is missing | 52 |
| b | needs a production entry (technique or new material) for a good it makes | 453 |
| c | provides a service with no market yet | 189 |
| d | sells nothing; should earn nothing directly | 515 |
| total | | 1209 |

Class counts above are after the hand review below (dated 2026-10-06). By rule alone they were a 55, b 367, c 172, d 615. The ranked work list, the per-cluster node counts and the D_enabling section further down were written before the review and still show the rule counts; the per-node table at the end carries the reviewed class.

## Hand review of class d and the flagged nodes (2026-10-06)

Test applied to each node: read name, note, pre, mat and category, then ask whether an operator of it sells a good or a service to someone outside the tree's own research chain. Finished stand-alone goods with a buyer (a bicycle, a telescope, a sextant, a cement kiln's cement, a loom attachment, a machine sold to firms) are b. A fee-for-use service (plating, assay, admission, imaging, trade margin) is c. Left in d: components of a vehicle, engine, loom or network (springs, brakes, valves, relays, microphones, boilers, signal gear), methods and standards (arches, trusses, foundations, grafting, voltage and gauge standards), sciences, research-only laboratory apparatus whose only buyer is another research node, institutions with no fee, and expeditions. Where a node was borderline it was moved out of d, because a wrong d deletes real earnings while a wrong b only keeps the typed figure until an entry is added.

Reviewed: all 615 class d nodes (612 in `data/branches/`, one in `62_control_ops_deep.json`, two in `mods/sample_solar_future_v9x3`), the four duplicate pairs named under "Judgement calls" (8 nodes), and the 44 `ag2_*` class b nodes (the "harvesting implement" call). Every branch file with class d nodes was covered, so no file is left unreviewed.

Result: 111 nodes left class d (94 to b, 17 to c); 11 nodes moved into d (3 duplicates from a, 8 from b). 504 class d nodes were confirmed d.

Reasons for confirming d on the contested groups: lens elements, eyepieces, prisms, condensers and mirrors are parts of an instrument; the instrument is the good. Photographic shutters, microphones, loudspeakers, relays, repeaters, telephone sets, transmitters and detectors are parts of a camera, set or network. Laboratory apparatus (cyclotron, mass spectrograph, diffraction cameras, wind tunnel, towing tank, test machines) is bought only by research. Rail, ship and motor-vehicle subsystems (couplers, brakes, boilers, valve gear, gearboxes, springs, rigging, signals) belong to a vehicle or a network that sells the carriage. Construction methods and structures (arches, trusses, dams, tunnels, foundations, cranes, scaffolding) are contractor techniques with no good of their own. Soil and plant methods (drainage, terracing, grafting, budding, layering, pollination) earn through crop yield. Standards (voltage, frequency, gauge, weights, scanning) and institutions without a fee (endowed chair, research institute, apprenticeship, factory organisation) sell nothing. Unmoved ag2 class b implements (reaper, mower, tedder, harrow, subsoiler, combine and similar) stay b: an implement maker sells machines to farms, as the tractor does. Remaining flagged duplicates: `pwr_peat`, `pwr_oil_refinery`, `pwr_coking` and `civ_wire_drawn` keep the entry (class a) and their twins drop to d.

Payback as the diagnostic computes it: `_total_cost / (rev - up)` (`sim/engine/node_payback_diagnostic.py`). Command: `python3 sim/simulator.py economy-check --payback --years 1` lists those under a quarter year (65 nodes, all authored; run on 2026-10-06 with the default civilisation `rome_100ad`). Over all authored nodes: under 0.25 years 65, under 1 year 301, no positive net 60.

Classification is by rule on node id and category (a rules file, not hand review of every node); the borderline calls are listed under "Judgement calls". Counts are exact for the rules; individual nodes may move between b and d on review.

## Ranked work list

Ranked by how badly the authored figure breaks the game (fastest payback in the group, then how many nodes under a quarter year), with the node count one data addition fixes. Class d is a deletion of authored `rev` (set to zero) with no new data, so it is the cheapest fix whatever its rank.

| Rank | Fix (cluster) | Class | Nodes | Fastest payback (years) | Median payback | Under 0.25 y | Under 1 y |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | C_electricity | c | 23 | 0.05555 | 0.6409 | 4 | 15 |
| 2 | B_conversion | b | 48 | 0.1159 | 0.2671 | 23 | 40 |
| 3 | D_enabling | d | 615 | 0.1265 | 2.645 | 35 | 127 |
| 4 | C_transport | c | 57 | 0.1587 | 2.784 | 2 | 12 |
| 5 | B_goods_new_material | b | 115 | 0.21 | 1.134 | 1 | 46 |
| 6 | A_bound | a | 19 | 0.2775 | 2.35 | 0 | 7 |
| 7 | B_goods_existing_inputs | b | 165 | 0.2888 | 2.353 | 0 | 35 |
| 8 | A_regate | a | 36 | 0.3125 | 2.303 | 0 | 11 |
| 9 | C_communication | c | 24 | 0.3974 | 3.789 | 0 | 2 |
| 10 | C_household_service | c | 43 | 0.5995 | 5.293 | 0 | 4 |
| 11 | B_capital_good | b | 39 | 0.7064 | 3.481 | 0 | 2 |
| 12 | C_finance | c | 25 | 2.029 | 14.5 | 0 | 0 |

Same list by nodes fixed per data addition: D_enabling (615), B_goods_existing_inputs (165), B_goods_new_material (115), C_transport (57), B_conversion (48), C_household_service (43), B_capital_good (39), A_regate (36), C_finance (25), C_communication (24), C_electricity (23), A_bound (19).

Recommended order of work:

1. **C_electricity** (23 nodes, fastest 0.05555 years, 4 under a quarter year). One electricity delivery market: electrical_mj sold at a customer's load, with a grid reach and loss, bought by lighting, motors, traction, electrochemistry.
2. **D_enabling** (615 nodes, fastest 0.1265 years, 35 under a quarter year). Nothing to add: stop earning authored revenue and let the supported entries carry the value.
3. **B_conversion** (48 nodes, fastest 0.1159 years, 23 under a quarter year). Technique entries in data/production/70_energy.json for mechanical_mj and electrical_mj (carriers exist and are priced), each with capital, service_life_years and annual_output_at_basis.
4. **C_transport** (57 nodes, fastest 0.1587 years, 2 under a quarter year). One carriage market: freight and fares priced from route cost (sim/geography routes and freight already exist), bought by traders, travellers, states.
5. **A_bound** (19 nodes, fastest 0.2775 years, 0 under a quarter year). Add a physical bound to the 19 nodes that already gate entries: capital with annual_output_at_basis (or labour_hours with staff, or annual_output_t) so node_output.output_baskets can run.
6. **A_regate** (36 nodes, fastest 0.3125 years, 0 under a quarter year). Point the existing entry's requires_node at this node (or list both gates) so the node's own output is what it earns; no new physics.
7. **C_communication** (24 nodes, fastest 0.3974 years, 0 under a quarter year). One communication market: message, call, broadcast and publication fees, bought by firms, states and households.
8. **C_household_service** (43 nodes, fastest 0.5995 years, 0 under a quarter year). Household and public service demand: care, schooling, lodging, entertainment, water, sewerage, defence; bought by households and states.
9. **C_finance** (25 nodes, fastest 2.029 years, 0 under a quarter year). Financial, insurance and trading services: fees and margins, bought by households and firms.
10. **B_goods_existing_inputs** (165 nodes, fastest 0.2888 years, 0 under a quarter year). New technique entries (labour_hours, inputs, capital, unit_dimension) for goods whose material or close substitute already exists; a process improvement is an entry for the same output with lower labour or plant.
11. **B_goods_new_material** (115 nodes, fastest 0.21 years, 1 under a quarter year). New material entries (and technique entries) for goods with no material yet: unit_dimension, unit_mass_kg for counts, inputs, labour_hours, yield_basis.
12. **B_capital_good** (39 nodes, fastest 0.7064 years, 0 under a quarter year). Capital goods as count materials with unit_mass_kg and a buyer: firms' own plant purchases (the capital lists already name the goods).

Rationale for the order: the electricity delivery nodes (`el2_three_wire_distribution_system`, `el2_ring_main_distribution`) are the two fastest paybacks in the whole tree, and one delivered-electricity market also settles the grid-operation nodes. Class d is a pure deletion and removes the most nodes with no design work. Conversion techniques (`B_conversion`) hold many of the next-fastest paybacks (`en_wind_electric`, `en_wind_pump`, the dynamos and turbines) and need only entries in an existing file for carriers that are already priced. Class a is cheap but small. Goods classes are large and slow (each good needs inputs, labour, a yield basis and a demand), and their paybacks are mostly slower than a quarter year, so they break the game least; the finance services are slowest of all.

## Data each fix needs, in production-schema terms

Schema reference: `data/production/_SCHEMA.md`. An entry states per batch `outputs`, `inputs`, `labour_hours`, `unit_dimension`, `basis`, `yield_basis`, `conf`, `requires_node` (the gate that makes `node_output.entries_gated_by` find it), optional `capital` (each with `build_materials`, `build_labour_hours`, `service_life_years`, `annual_output_at_basis`, `capital_basis`) and, for energy, `thermal_mj`, `mechanical_mj`, `electrical_mj`. Derivation (`sim/engine/node_output.py`) bounds output by plant `annual_output_at_basis`, by staff hours (`sch` + `art`) over the entry hours, or by a declared `annual_output_t`; a node with none of these stays unbounded and falls back to authored.

### C_electricity (class c, 23 nodes)

One electricity delivery market, defined once. Service: electrical_mj delivered at a customer's load. Buyers: households (`hom_electric_lighting`), firms (motors via `mechanical_mj_motor`, electrolysis and arc entries already read `electrical_mj`), traction (`tr_electric_locomotive`, trams). Data: a delivery step in `70_energy.json` (transmission and distribution losses and a capital list for lines and substations with `annual_output_at_basis`), a grid reach (which tiles a network serves; `sim/geography`), and a demand side beyond the entries that already consume `electrical_mj`. Without the demand side the market is only intermediate; with it, the nodes here earn a wheeling margin derived from line capital and loss.

Fastest paybacks here: `el2_three_wire_distribution_system` (0.05555 y), `el2_ring_main_distribution` (0.06913 y), `en_grid_interconnection` (0.2229 y), `en_power_factor_correction` (0.2309 y), `en_transmission_line` (0.2678 y), `en_battery_charging` (0.2797 y), `el2_substation_voltage_regulation` (0.3725 y), `en_hydroelectric_station` (0.442 y).

### D_enabling (class d, 615 nodes)

No data to add. Set authored `rev` to zero (or mark `kind` SCIENCE where it is knowledge) and let the entries that depend on the node carry the value through lower `labour_hours`, lower plant, or a new technique. Instruments, structural methods, components, institutions of learning and expeditions belong here.

Fastest paybacks here: `el2_electric_locomotive_traction_motor` (0.1265 y), `tr_submarine_hull` (0.1344 y), `tr_welded_hull` (0.1473 y), `en_frequency_standardisation` (0.1598 y), `tr_locomotive_boiler` (0.1646 y), `en_separate_condenser` (0.1701 y), `tr_catenary_overhead` (0.1788 y), `tr_reduction_gearing` (0.1884 y).

### B_conversion (class b, 48 nodes)

One technique entry per prime mover or generator in `data/production/70_energy.json`, keyed like the existing `mechanical_mj_waterwheel` / `electrical_mj_dynamo` entries: `outputs` the carrier (`mechanical_mj_<technique>` or `electrical_mj_<technique>`), `inputs` the fuel or head, `capital` list with `annual_output_at_basis`, `requires_node` the node. The carriers and the grading mechanism (`sim/engine/energy_prices.py`) exist; the same entry also lets the solver pick the cheapest technique, so the node earns by selling energy at the market price rather than by a typed number. Wind (`en_post_mill`, `en_wind_electric`, `en_wind_pump`) needs a wind energy source that the energy entries do not yet state (Complaint 283 names it); tide (`en_tide_mill`) likewise.

Fastest paybacks here: `en_wind_electric` (0.1159 y), `en_wind_pump` (0.134 y), `en_jet_engine` (0.1664 y), `en_fourneyron_turbine` (0.171 y), `en_francis_turbine` (0.1719 y), `en_gas_turbine` (0.1731 y), `en_kaplan_turbine` (0.179 y), `en_undershot_wheel` (0.1805 y).

### C_transport (class c, 57 nodes)

One carriage market. Service: tonne-kilometres and passenger-kilometres between tiles. Freight and routes already exist in `sim/geography` and `sim/economy/good_mass.py` (the schema says "transport not modelled anywhere" for production entries, which is stale in that freight is costed in geography): the missing piece is that carriers sell capacity. Buyers: traders moving goods, states moving troops and mail, households travelling. Data: an entry-like record per vehicle or crossing with `capital` (vehicle build bill, `service_life_years`, annual tonne-kilometres as `annual_output_at_basis`), `labour_hours` per trip, an energy draw (`mechanical_mj` or `thermal_mj`), and a gate. Tolls and ports (`fin_toll_bridge`, `fin_ferry`, `civ_harbour_dock`) earn a share of the traffic crossing, which needs the traffic count per route.

Fastest paybacks here: `tr_articulated_locomotive` (0.1587 y), `tr_canal_lift` (0.1707 y), `tr_diesel_electric` (0.3534 y), `tr_hopper_wagon` (0.4297 y), `tr_electric_locomotive` (0.5123 y), `air_helicopter_rotor` (0.703 y), `tr_dredger` (0.7239 y), `air_flying_boat` (0.7301 y).

### A_bound (class a, 19 nodes)

For each of the 19: add `capital` to the entry (a plant with `annual_output_at_basis`), or `labour_hours` with the node staff (`sch`, `art`), or a single-product `annual_output_t` where the entry has one output in kilograms. Where the reason is "adds less than staff cost", the entry needs real `labour_hours` and `inputs` so value added is positive (that is a pricing fault to check, not only a missing bound). Extraction nodes (`pwr_coal_seam`, `pwr_peat`, `met_froth_flotation`, `pwr_nuclear_fission`) are bounded by a deposit or parent stream: state the deposit, not a plant. Nursery lines (`ag2_hopping`, `ag2_pyrethrum`) are bounded by land (`land_hectare_years`).

Fastest paybacks here: `med_opium_mandrake` (0.2775 y), `mirror_amalgam` (0.4172 y), `chm_caustic_soda` (0.5339 y), `chm_superphosphate` (0.5462 y), `chm_glycerol` (0.647 y), `chm_solvay_process` (0.7006 y), `gunpowder` (0.7499 y), `chm_polyethylene` (1.088 y).

### A_regate (class a, 36 nodes)

No new physics. For each node the existing entry (named in the table) must name this node as a gate: either change `requires_node`, or add a list gate (a `requires_any_node` style field would need a loader change, which is a code task not covered here). Where an entry has no gate (`requires_node: null`) a null means no technology is needed, so repointing it would make the technology gate something the founder can already do: check each before changing. Duplicate nodes that describe one process (for example `pwr_oil_refinery` and `en_oil_refining_distillation`, `pwr_peat` and `en_peat_fuel`) should keep one revenue-bearing node and drop the other to class d.

Fastest paybacks here: `en_oil_refining_distillation` (0.3125 y), `en_coal_mining_washing` (0.375 y), `chm_ammonia_recovery` (0.5364 y), `hom_shampoo_soap_based` (0.593 y), `chm_potash_mining` (0.6374 y), `chm_activated_carbon` (0.64 y), `pwr_coking` (0.7766 y), `en_peat_fuel` (0.8014 y).

### C_communication (class c, 24 nodes)

One communication market. Service: a message, a call, a broadcast minute, a printed notice. Buyers: firms (`com_stock_ticker`, `fin_advertising`), states (post, telegraph), households. Data: per-message labour and line capital (`if_submarine_cable_gutta_percha`, exchanges), a message volume that follows population and trade, and a price per message cleared against it. Print publications (`fin_newspaper_business`, `fin_almanac`, `fin_directory`) are goods once the printed-page good exists (`B_goods_existing_inputs`), so they move there.

Fastest paybacks here: `el2_telephone_exchange_switching_network` (0.3974 y), `radio` (0.9992 y), `com_broadcasting_institution` (1.112 y), `com_stock_ticker` (1.414 y), `com_signal_flags` (1.641 y), `com_telephone_manual_exchange` (1.702 y), `prn_newspaper_institution` (1.768 y), `telegraph_electric` (2.687 y).

### C_household_service (class c, 43 nodes)

Care, schooling, lodging, entertainment, water, sewerage, defence. Buyers: households (fees from `sim/world/` demand) and states (tax-funded). Data: a per-head service demand tied to population and income, staff (`sch`, `art`) hours as the only physical input, `capital` for buildings and plant, and for public goods (defence, sewerage) a state purchaser. Warships (`mil_*`) sell security to a state; with no state purchaser they should move to d.

Fastest paybacks here: `med_ligature_haemostasis` (0.5995 y), `med_wound_suturing` (0.7018 y), `med_obstetric_practice` (0.7895 y), `med_bone_setting` (0.7936 y), `med_saline_resuscitation` (1.24 y), `med_spectacles_refraction` (1.242 y), `med_nursing_profession` (1.654 y), `med_vaccination_progression` (2.344 y).

### C_finance (class c, 25 nodes)

Financial and trading services. Service: credit, deposit safekeeping, insurance cover, brokerage, trade margin. Buyers: households and firms. Data: the interest rate and risk already in the economy (`starting_interest_rate`, the civilisation market rate), a deposit and loan book per bank, premiums set from loss rates (needs an event-loss record), and for trading nodes the margin between buying and selling prices the price solver already computes. These are the nodes with the least physical content: derive from the credit and trade flows the engine already tracks rather than from `production/`.

Fastest paybacks here: `endowment_land` (2.029 y), `fin_pawnshop` (4.033 y), `fin_marine_insurance` (6.815 y), `fin_deposit_bank` (6.99 y), `fin_trading_post` (8.428 y), `fin_fire_insurance` (8.765 y), `fin_futures` (10.17 y), `fin_reinsurance` (10.25 y).

### B_goods_existing_inputs (class b, 165 nodes)

New entry (or a lower-labour variant of an existing entry for the same output, which `concern_volume.entries_held_for` already lets compete) per good: processed foods and drinks, cloth and garments, printed pages, household goods. The input materials mostly exist (`milk_kg`, `wheat_kg`, `paper_kg`, `ink_kg`, `thread_kg`, `fabric_kg`, `cloth_kg`, `sugar`, `leather_kg`, `glass_raw_kg`); the output good (butter, canned food, a printed book, a garment, a lamp) usually has no material, so each also needs `unit_dimension`, `unit_mass_kg` for count goods, and a demand (`sim/world/` demand) so a price is cleared. Harvest and threshing machines (`ag2_reaper`, `ag2_threshing_machine`, `ag2_combine_harvester`, ...) are labour-saving variants of the existing crop entries (`wheat_kg`, `hectare_land`), not separate goods: they need a lower `labour_hours` variant gated on the node.

Fastest paybacks here: `hom_safety_razor` (0.2888 y), `hom_lamp_argand` (0.4053 y), `hom_toys_dolls` (0.4486 y), `tex_flying_shuttle` (0.4726 y), `hom_deodorant` (0.4789 y), `tex_horizontal_loom` (0.5001 y), `hom_button` (0.51 y), `tex_sewing_machine` (0.5239 y).

### B_goods_new_material (class b, 115 nodes)

New material entries for goods the tree has no material for: chemicals (`chm_*`: sulfuric acid by the contact route, oleum, nitric acid by Ostwald, TNT, dynamite, aspirin, nylon, bakelite), pesticides and fertilisers (`ag2_*`), refined fuels (kerosene, petrol, lubricating oil; `petroleum_refined_kg` is one lumped good today) and electronic components (valves, capacitors, resistors, inductors as `count` goods with `unit_mass_kg`). Each needs `inputs` from existing materials (`sulfur_kg`, `ammonia_kg`, `phenol_kg`, `nitric_acid_kg`, `lead_kg`, ...), `labour_hours`, `capital` where the plant matters, a `yield_basis` that survives a chemist, and a second route to an existing good needs its own key (one key per output).

Fastest paybacks here: `en_fuel_injection` (0.21 y), `el2_xray_tube_high_voltage_cathode_rays` (0.2511 y), `en_gas_holder` (0.3193 y), `chm_dynamite` (0.4123 y), `el2_photodiode_photocell_selenium` (0.4351 y), `el2_travelling_wave_tube_linear_amplifier` (0.4683 y), `pwr_fuel_oil` (0.4773 y), `en_liquid_propellant` (0.5039 y).

### B_capital_good (class b, 39 nodes)

Machine tools, bearings, tyres, structural sections: count materials with `unit_mass_kg`, an entry with `inputs` (steel, rubber, cast iron) and `labour_hours`, and a buyer. The only buyer is another firm's capital list, which names capital goods as `build_materials` (not as a market good), so these need either a capital-goods market or to stay class d (their value is the cheaper plant elsewhere). Treat as d unless a capital-goods market is built.

Fastest paybacks here: `prc_automatic_screw_machine` (0.7064 y), `prc_capstan_turret_lathe` (0.9357 y), `tl_pneumatic_tyre` (1.521 y), `prc_tap_die` (1.805 y), `tl_inner_tube` (1.914 y), `tl_plain_bearing` (1.95 y), `tl_spoked_wheel` (2.077 y), `tl_iron_tyre` (2.12 y).

## Judgement calls and limits

- Classes are assigned by rule on node id and category. Some b nodes are really d (a harvesting implement is a labour-saving variant of a crop entry, which is a technique entry only if a lower-labour variant is authored) and some d nodes (printing apparatus, instruments for sale) could be goods. Move a node by editing the rule in the generating script, not the report.
- A prime mover or generator is b, not c, because the carriers it sells (`mechanical_mj`, `electrical_mj`) are already priced; electricity delivery (lines, substations, storage, grid operation) is c.
- Components of a vehicle, engine or network (rail couplings, valves, governors, insulators, fuses, breakers, bearings in a hull) are d: the vehicle or network, not the part, sells carriage or energy. They hold many of the fast paybacks, so deleting their authored `rev` is the quickest way to remove the worst offenders.
- Sciences and measurement apparatus are d. `economy-check --payback` also shows sciences already derived as knowledge (earn nothing); the ten knowledge nodes are not part of the authored count.
- Payback here uses the loaded figures for the default civilisation, `rome_100ad`; other civilisations re-derive wages and may shift individual paybacks (`node_revenue.for_civilisation`), but authored `rev` is the same typed figure in every civilisation (it does not follow wages or prices), which is part of the fault.
- Duplicates: several nodes describe one process (for example `pwr_peat` and `en_peat_fuel`, `pwr_oil_refinery` and `en_oil_refining_distillation`, `pwr_coking` and `en_coke_oven`, `civ_wire_drawn` and `cn_wire_cable_spinning`). One should carry the output; the others should drop to d.

## Method (reproduce)

```
python3 sim/simulator.py economy-check --help
python3 sim/simulator.py economy-check --payback --years 1
python3 -c "import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'.'); from sim.engine import data; _t,_p,nodes,_w,_g = data.load(); \
  rows=[(i,n['kind'],n['rev'],n['up'],n['cap'],n['_total_cost']) for i,n in nodes.items() if n.get('_revenue_basis')=='authored']; print(len(rows))"
```

Gated authored nodes: `sim.engine.node_output.entries_gated_by(node_id, sim.labour.api.production_data())` is non-empty for 19 of them. Production entries: 320 keys in `data/production/` at the time of the audit (`python3 -c` over `production_data()`). Classification rules are in the audit session scratch files and are summarised above; re-run the loader command to refresh counts.
